Declaratory Judgment + $275K in Attorney Fees in Settlement of N.H. "XX" Wristbands Case

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Mostly law professors | Sometimes contrarian | Often libertarian | Always independent

The Institute for Free Speech, which litigated the case, reports on the settlement agreement:

Take off the wristbands or leave. That was the message Bow school officials gave parents and a grandfather who were silently protesting in support of female-only sports by wearing pink "XX" wristbands at a girls' high school soccer game in 2024…. After more than two years of litigation …, the Bow School District has agreed to a judgment declaring that it violated the plaintiffs' rights by barring them from wearing the wristbands at the game. U.S…. The settlement also provides $275,000 in attorneys' fees and costs.

The judgment declares that the plaintiffs "have a First Amendment right to silently wear pink wristbands bearing the XX symbol on the sidelines, in the spectator's area, or in the parking lot of any Bow District sporting event." It binds the district and its Board of Education, and it remains in effect even if the district amends its policies….

The plaintiffs wore the wristbands, whose "XX" symbols reference female chromosome structure, to protest a policy permitting a biological male to compete on the opposing girls' team. School officials, accompanied by a police officer, confronted them. The referee halted play until one spectator removed his wristband, and school officials later issued trespass orders barring Fellers and Andy Foote from school grounds, preventing them from attending their children's games and other school activities for a time….

After the district court denied a preliminary injunction in April 2025, the plaintiffs appealed. In August 2026, the U.S. Court of Appeals for the First Circuit ruled in the plaintiffs' favor, finding that they "have established a likelihood of success as to their viewpoint discrimination claim." The court concluded that the protest "fell short of harassment and disruption," and that school officials "cannot favor one side of a debate involving parental speech on an issue of prominent public concern." …

Here's my August post excerpting the First Circuit's opinion, written by First Circuit Judge Sandra Lynch, joined by Judge Jeffrey Howard:

On September 17, 2024, [plaintiffs] were instructed by Bow High School officials to remove pink "XX" wristbands they wore in protest while watching their daughters (in [one plaintiff's] case, granddaughter) play in a girls' soccer game against a team with one transgender athlete. Further sanctions followed…. [W]e conclude that appellants have established a likelihood of success as to their viewpoint discrimination claim arising from the sanctions imposed on them for their passive speech in wearing these wristbands….

The district court analyzed the school playing fields and adjacent parking lot, as appellants requested, under the mode of First Amendment law applicable to limited public fora as to impermissible viewpoint discrimination…. [The] appellee school district argues that this case must be analyzed more in the mode of Tinker v. Des Moines Independent Community School District (1969) … [and other cases that] recognize the particular interests inherent in the public elementary and high school settings, and the necessary discretion given to public school administrators in the difficult job of running public schools….

Neither of those two different classic modes of First Amendment analysis, urged on by these opposing parties, is a perfect fit for this public high school speech case, and we note that the Supreme Court has not addressed this precise question. We need not and do not reach the complex question of which (or whether) either of these alternate modes is the appropriate First Amendment type of analytical model for the facts of record here. We conclude that on these facts the differences in analysis would not affect the outcome here….

The school's legitimate interest in regulating certain kinds of speech on the playing field sidelines … is not demonstrated by the evidence in this record. There is no evidence that appellants' protest caused a material disruption [which would be relevant under Tinker]. There was no disruption until school officials chose to intervene, after which the referee suspended the game for approximately fifteen minutes and the school officials chose to impose restrictions on appellants' speech and impose sanctions. The appellants did not stage any pregame demonstrations; did not march, shout, chant, or otherwise disrupt the game; and did not confront Tirrell or any other player. Instead, they waited until halftime before donning the "XX" wristbands in a passive, silent gesture of protest. There is also no evidence of harassment, given there is no evidence in the record that any player actually saw the wristbands.

The lack of any evidence demonstrating harassment also defeats the school district's argument that it had an affirmative duty under Title IX to protect students from the speech in which appellants engaged…. While it is true that appellants chose to protest at the September 17 game because of Tirrell being a player, the record does not contain evidence of appellants engaging in harassment before or during the game. To the contrary, appellants did not shout, chant, make threats, or aggressively gesture at Tirrell to communicate their views against her participation. Nor is there evidence that Tirrell or any other player even saw the wristbands or "subjectively perceived the environment to be hostile or abusive."

Principal Fisk did note the challenges that transgender students face generally. But Fisk did not ever explain how appellants' display of the "XX" symbol constituted sufficiently severe or pervasive conduct to qualify as harassment under Title IX. And to the extent the district court concluded that the silent wearing of "XX" wristbands on the sidelines of a school soccer field, without more, always amounts to harassing speech, we disagree.

The school district responds that it may make "predictive judgment[s]" to proactively prohibit conduct that it believes will eventually lead to an actionable Title IX claim….. We agree with the general proposition. But the argument fails on the facts of this record…. We need not decide in this appeal whether and under what circumstances passive speech, on other facts, consisting of displaying the "XX" symbol on school property during school events could be reasonably thought to be harassing. But appellants' silent display of the "XX" symbol at the September 17 game fell short of harassment and disruption, and we conclude that the district court erred in holding that the school's interest in prevention of harassment justified the prohibition.

While it is a closer issue, we conclude that the school district likely was not justified in sanctioning the appellants for their passive speech based on its concerns derived from the earlier exchanges with the appellants and other parents. None of appellants' reported discussions, including their possible different methods of protest reported to Bow High School officials by other parents, led to any attempt to "heckle and intimidate" Tirrell. And that was evident both before and throughout the game.

It is true that Fellers initially attempted to convince his daughter to get the Bow High School players to wear the wristbands, but his daughter informed him that "she could not get full support from the team and was afraid that it would divide them." Fellers's Facebook post made the night before the game encouraged spectators "to protect the integrity and safety of female athletics" and "come out to support our XX Lady Falcons and show your solidarity with our girls' team." But the record shows only that the appellants and perhaps others of similar views engaged in a passive protest and only started the protest at halftime. While the school district may make predictive judgments about whether disruption may occur and "need not be certain of its forecast," it cannot use those projections to immediately suppress a passive, silent protest, that did not impair any of the legitimate school administration interests recognized by the Supreme Court.

This brings us to the school officials' other stated reasons for sanctioning appellants. Superintendent Kelley stated that the "XX" symbol conveyed an "exclusionary" message about gender. Principal Fisk, who ordered appellants to remove the wristbands, described the symbol both as "hateful" and as "anti-trans." As to these justifications for the sanctions imposed, the record evidence demonstrates the likelihood that the school district imposed sanctions on the appellants' wearing of the wristbands because it disagreed with the protest message conveyed….

Although the leading Supreme Court case on viewpoint discrimination in academic limited public fora, involved colleges and not high schools, and involved a limited public forum, both significant differences, Rosenberger's anguage about viewpoint discrimination is pertinent. "The government must abstain from regulating speech when the specific motivating ideology or the opinion or perspective of the speaker is the rationale for the restriction."

The characteristics of the school as a place of learning reinforce that administrators cannot favor one side of a debate involving parental speech on an issue of prominent public concern, and which, in those parents' views, concerned the safety of their children. As the Supreme Court stated in a case involving a high school football game, "learning how to tolerate diverse expressive activities has always been 'part of learning how to live in a pluralistic society.'"

Judge Julie Rikelman concurred in the judgment, reasoning that Tinker might not prohibit viewpoint-discriminatory speech restrictions, but that in this case the school district had accepted the viewpoint neutrality requirement:

The school district accepted that the limited public forum framework applied to the facts here and conceded repeatedly, both in its brief and at oral argument, that it could restrict speech at the September 17 soccer game only for viewpoint neutral reasons…. Although it did urge us to conduct our First Amendment analysis with the principles of Tinker in mind, it did so only in arguing that its actions were reasonable and thus satisfied the [reasonableness] prong of the limited public forum test….

With the school district's concession in mind, I agree that the record lacks evidence of actionable harassment or material disruption from the plaintiffs' speech at the September 17 soccer game. The record also demonstrates that school officials likely restricted the XX symbol because they viewed it as "exclusionary" and demeaning to transgender students. That amounts to viewpoint discrimination under relevant precedent.

Del Kolde, Brett Nolan, and Nathan Ristuccia (Institute for Free Speech) and Richard Lehmann (Lehmann Major List, PLLC) represent plaintiffs. were on brief, for appellants.

How Octopuses Lock in Long-Term Memory Without New Proteins

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

A neurobiological investigation published in the Journal of Neuroscience reveals a distinct molecular mechanism underpinning long-term memory in the octopus brain. Led by researchers from the Hebrew University of Jerusalem and Ruppin Academic Center, the study demonstrates that long-term potentiation (LTP) in the octopus vertical lobe is preserved via an autonomous, self-sustaining nitric oxide (NO) feedback loop.

Unlike classical vertebrate memory consolidation, which requires nuclear gene expression and de novo protein synthesis, the octopus system continuously reactivates nitric oxide synthase to maintain enhanced neurotransmitter release, revealing an alternative evolutionary solution for enduring memory storage.

Octopuses are renowned for their problem-solving abilities, associative learning, exploratory navigation, and tool manipulation. Yet their evolutionary lineage diverged from vertebrates over 500 million years ago, leading to a radically decentralized nervous system where two-thirds of their neurons reside within the arms rather than the central brain capsule.

In mammalian neurobiology, stabilizing long-term memories requires long-term potentiation (LTP), the persistent strengthening of chemical synapses following high-frequency activation. A dogma of vertebrate neuroscience is that transforming early, fleeting LTP into late, enduring memory traces requires transcription factors, nuclear signaling, and the translation of new structural proteins to physically remodel synaptic junctions.

In a study published in the Journal of Neuroscience, neurobiologists Dr. Ana Luiza Turchetti-Maia, Dr. Tal Shomrat, and Prof. Binyamin (Benny) Hochner investigated the vertical lobe of the octopus brain—a cerebral structure functionally analogous to the mammalian hippocampus.

Their findings show that octopuses preserve learned synaptic memory through an autonomous gaseous feedback loop that bypasses the need for new protein production.

The octopus vertical lobe acts as the primary sensory-associative hub for visual and tactile learning. When vertical lobe neural circuits receive strong, brief electrical stimulation, the connections between nerve cells undergo robust long-term potentiation, reinforcing the synaptic pathways that register past experience.

However, electrophysiological and biochemical assays revealed that the downstream cascade sustaining this potentiation differs fundamentally from vertebrate models.

The mechanism centers on nitric oxide (NO), a gaseous, membrane-permeable signaling molecule:

This reciprocal activation establishes a self-reinforcing, regenerative enzymatic circuit. Once switched “ON” by an initial burst of activity, the loop keeps synaptic transmission enhanced over long time horizons.

The key divergence from mammalian biology emerged when the team exposed vertical lobe slices to protein synthesis inhibitors.

In the vertebrate hippocampus, blocking ribosomal translation reliably extinguishes late-phase LTP within hours, erasing consolidated memory storage. In the octopus vertical lobe, blocking protein synthesis had no effect on the maintenance of synaptic potentiation.

The self-perpetuating enzymatic cycle of NOS and NO was sufficient to maintain strengthened synaptic transmission independently, challenging long-held assumptions that long-term cellular memories strictly require structural protein synthesis.

“What makes this discovery so exciting is that octopuses have evolved a fundamentally different molecular solution to a challenge shared by all learning systems: how to turn brief experience into a long-lasting change in neural connections,” explains Prof. Benny Hochner of the Hebrew University. “It is a remarkable example of how evolution can give rise to similar principles of learning and memory through very different biological mechanisms.”

The discovery provides insights into evolutionary neurobiology. Because cephalopods developed complex cognition entirely independently of vertebrates, their neural machinery represents an alternative blueprint for computational intelligence.

Understanding non-protein-dependent memory mechanisms broadens the theoretical framework of neurobiology, offering new models for synthetic neural networks, bio-inspired computation, and the study of non-canonical plasticity pathways across diverse animal phyla.

How does memory maintenance in the octopus differ from vertebrates?

In vertebrates, long-term memory consolidation in the hippocampus requires new protein synthesis to physically remodel synapses. In the octopus vertical lobe, long-term potentiation is maintained through a self-sustaining nitric oxide feedback loop that operates without synthesizing new proteins.

What role does nitric oxide (NO) play in the octopus brain?

Nitric oxide acts as a dual-function molecular switch. It facilitates neurotransmitter release at the synapse and continuously reactivates its own producing enzyme, nitric oxide synthase (NOS), locking the synapse in a strengthened state over time.

What is the vertical lobe in the octopus brain?

The vertical lobe is the central learning and memory center of the octopus brain. It is the functional analog of the vertebrate hippocampus, responsible for processing sensory experiences and supporting long-term potentiation.

A novel nitric oxide (NO)-dependent ‘molecular switch’ mediates LTP in the Octopus vulgaris brain through persistent activation of nitric oxide synthase (NOS)

Cephalopod mollusks provide a striking example of the independent evolution of advanced cognitive abilities in invertebrates. In octopuses, learning and memory are mediated by the vertical lobe (VL), whose visual input synapses undergo robust activity-dependent long-term potentiation (LTP).

Here, we used Octopus vulgaris of either sex to show that persistent activation of nitric oxide synthase (NOS) mediates both LTP expression and maintenance, independently of de novo protein synthesis. Contrary to the prevailing view, NO signaling is not required for LTP induction, as LTP can still be induced in the presence of NOS inhibitors.

Remarkably, however, NOS inhibitors blocked presynaptic LTP expression, while NO donors induced only short-term potentiation, together suggesting that LTP expression depends on persistently elevated NO levels. The effective NO concentrations, estimated both pharmacologically and amperometrically, were more than three orders of magnitude higher than those required for activation of the canonical sGC–cGMP pathway, suggesting that NO acts through direct covalent modification of proteins, such as S-nitrosylation.

In a subset of synapses, the NOS inhibitor L-NNA rapidly and completely erased established LTP; however, following washout, full potentiation could be reinstated by renewed tetanic stimulation. We therefore propose a ‘molecular switch’ mechanism in which an NO-dependent positive feedback loop persistently reactivates NOS, thereby maintaining LTP.

These findings reveal an unprecedented form of convergent evolution in which molecular innovations equip the octopus VL network with the computational properties required for associative learning.

Trump reversed the Pied Piper’s bargain, and voters stiffed him

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

When the pied piper loses his audience, we must ask whether the people have rediscovered their conscience or simply found a new tune to follow.

On Tuesday night, Donald Trump and his MAGA movement saw what happens when political influence collides with the everyday realities of the people it seeks to lead.

The 2026 midterm elections brought a dramatic shift in political power. Democrats won control of both the House and the Senate, flipping Senate seats in states such as Texas and Kansas, which were long thought to be out of reach, and winning House districts Trump easily carried just two years ago.Â

Voter polls placed the presidentâs approval at approximately 38%, with roughly six in 10 voters saying he was a factor in their vote. But beneath those numbers lies a more consequential story. Americans are confronting the cost of living, the economic consequences of tariffs, the uncertainty of war with Iran and diminishing confidence in American leadership abroad.

Trump promised his voters he would drain the swamp, end foreign wars and bring prices down. Yet many Americans went to the polls questioning whether those promises had been kept. Unlike the Pied Piper of Hamelin, who rid the town of its rats, Trump now faces an electorate concerned that the problems he promised to eliminate have instead multiplied.Â

In the original story, the Piper fulfilled his bargain. He played his pipe, led the rats into the river and freed the town. But when the townspeople refused to pay him, he returned to exact revenge by leading away their children.Â

Over and over again throughout the midterm campaign, Trump urged supporters to vote as though he were personally on the ballot. In this telling, the Piper did not eliminate the rats. He added to them, and still he expected to be paid.

Critics argue that Trump reversed that bargain. Rather than delivering the relief promised to ordinary Americans, the administration has left many struggling with economic uncertainty while continuing to demand political loyalty. Over and over again throughout the midterm campaign, Trump urged supporters to vote as though he were personally on the ballot. In this telling, the Piper did not eliminate the rats. He added to them, and still he expected to be paid.Â Â

Trumpâs supporters see a different story. They point to immigration raids and mass deportations, deregulation and challenges to established political institutions as evidence that the president delivered on important commitments. From their perspective, voters who rejected those policies after experiencing the consequences resemble townspeople who accepted the Piperâs services, but refused to honor their bargain.Â

Both interpretations expose a fundamental ethical question: When does political loyalty cease to be an expression of shared principles and become an abdication of independent judgment?Â

In a democracy, voters are not obligated to follow the music indefinitely. They retain the right to question the destination, reconsider promises and withdraw their support. Political loyalty is not a debt citizens owe their leaders. It is a trust that leaders must continually earn.

Albert Hirschman, an American economist and author of âExit, Voice, and Loyalty: Responses to Decline in Firms, Organizations, and States,â distinguished between voice (working to change an organization from within) and exit (abandoning it). A vote against an incumbent is often itself an act of a voice. But where voters withdrew their allegiance from a party or movement altogether, Tuesday looked more like an exit. Hirschman saw loyalty as what keeps members inside, speaking up rather than leaving. His framework suggests that when loyalty silences voice instead, exit becomes the only remaining option.Â

But elected officials carry a different and greater responsibility.Â

Like the children of Hamelin, many Republican lawmakers followed the music toward an uncertain destination. Unlike those children, however, they had the authority â and the responsibility â to question where they were being led. Some embraced the presidentâs direction; others followed him despite reservations, knowing that dissent could invite a primary challenge. After all, they had seen others who challenged his leadership pay a considerable political cost. Kentucky Rep. Thomas Massie and Louisiana Sen. Bill Cassidy lost their Republican primaries to Trump-backed challengers.Â

Political cooperation is essential to governing. But cooperation becomes ethically troubling when loyalty to an individual displaces the obligation to question, deliberate and act independently. The oath of office is not a pledge of allegiance to a president, a party or a political movement. It is a commitment to uphold the Constitution. A president can command attention, share public debate and place enormous pressure on members of his own party. But the ability to demand loyalty does not create a moral entitlement to receive it.Â

The children of Hamelinâs Pied Piper tale had no part in the broken bargain, yet they paid its price. In our democracy, ordinary Americans and future generations often inherit the consequences of political decisions, and today they are paying a price. Economic instability, weakened public trust and the costs of war do not disappear when an election ends. A leaderâs success cannot be measured solely by the number of people willing to follow along with them. It must also be measured by what happens to those who never chase the tune.Â

Want more sharp takes on politics? Sign up for our free newsletter, Standing Room Only, written by Amanda Marcotte, now also a weekly show on YouTube or wherever you get your podcasts.

The election has shifted power in Washington. Democrats, after winning sizable, historic victories throughout the country, will soon have an opportunity to exercise congressional oversight and reinforce the institutional independence our constitutional system requires.Â

But changing who holds the instrument does not necessarily change the music.Â

Democrats now face their own ethical test. Will they exercise power with the transparency, accountability and respect for constitutional checks they demanded from their opponents? Will they welcome dissent within their own ranks? And will they resist the temptation to substitute partisan loyalty for independent judgment?Â Â

An election can change the balance of power overnight. It cannot, by itself, restore public trust or guarantee ethical leadership. That requires leaders willing to place constitutional responsibility above political convenience, institutions prepared to exercise meaningful oversight, and citizens who refuse to surrender their judgment to the most compelling voice in the room. The first six months of the 120th Congress must be defined not merely by a change in political power, but by a demonstrable commitment to ethical reform, transparency, and accountability. The American people did not vote simply for a change in leadership. They voted for a government worthy of their trust.

The Pied Piperâs greatest power was not simply his ability to play a captivating tune. It was his ability to persuade others to follow without questioning where their journey would end.Â

Political ethicist Dennis Thompson called this the problem of many hands: When many officials each contribute a small part to a collective outcome, responsibility seems to dissolve until no one bears it. So, when political loyalty becomes more important than independent judgment, who bears responsibility â the leader who plays the tune, or those who willingly follow? Thompsonâs framework does not allow individual lawmakers to hide behind presidential leadership, party discipline or collective decisions. Nor does it assign blame equally. Responsibility depends on each officialâs authority, knowledge, contribution and the alternatives available to them. By that measure, those who knew better and had the power to act cannot claim they were merely following the music from the leader in the red hat.

When the music finally stops, the question is not simply who will lead next.Â

It is whether we have learned to listen differently.

10 Signs You’re Falling Out of Love With Your Partner—And What to do Next, According to Relationship Experts

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Here's how to tell if you should call it quits or save your 'ship.

Women's Health may earn commission from the links on this page, but we only feature products we believe in. Why Trust Us?

You feel a rush every time their name pops up on your phone. You can’t stop thinking about them. You’re counting down the hours until the next time you see each other. You’ve fallen in love, and it's “that can't-eat, can't-sleep, reach-for-the-stars, over-the-fence, World Series kind of stuff.” (IYKYK—and when it comes to love, you know.)

Or at least, you used to feel that way. But lately, you’ve started daydreaming about the sexy stranger you see on the train every morning, your partner’s once-adorable habits have become downright annoying, or you feel more like roommates than soulmates. If any of this sounds familiar, you might be falling out of love.

Rest assured, “it’s normal for partners to feel a sense of ‘falling out of love’ now and again,” says Carla Marie Manly, PhD, a clinical psychologist and author of The Joy of Imperfect Love. Relationships naturally change over time, and your feelings can ebb and flow along with those changes.

Often, a change in those warm-fuzzy feels is due to a variety of factors, rather than a single issue, says Nikki Coleman, PhD, a licensed psychologist and sex self-confidence coach based in Texas. Some common causes could be mental health challenges, a shift in personal values, financial stress, parenting duties, or time spent away from each other due to work. Any and/or a combination of these factors can lead a couple to drift apart, even though neither partner has done anything “wrong.”

Falling out of love doesn’t necessarily mean it’s time to call it quits—instead, it might be an opportunity to reassess the relationship and rekindle your connection. But first, here's how to tell if you've lost that loving feeling:

Think back to the early stages of dating: You showed up to a date, eager to learn everything you could about your new love interest. When there is a lack of curiosity—maybe you feel like you already know everything about your partner, for example—it’s a big sign you could be falling out of love, Manly says.

Unlike the honeymoon phase when enthusiasm is heightened, falling out of love is often marked by a sense of apathy or ambivalence. This is known as “relationship fatigue,” meaning you feel drained and have less motivation to keep the connection going, says Kalley Hartman, LMFT, a licensed marriage and family therapist in Newport Beach, California. “Over time, we may start to feel overwhelmed by our relationships, leading us to become tired or apathetic about investing energy in them.”

Relationship fatigue can look like being disengaged from your partner, generally uninterested in them and the relationship, or even cynical about your future together. You may forget important milestones, stop prioritizing quality time together, or feel like putting in effort is a chore. “Partners who are falling out of love often stop making plans,” Manly says. “A partner who shows no interest in planning future vacations or creating shared goals may be signaling that they have one foot out the door.”

“This may show up as avoiding conversations, not making time to spend together, or having difficulty communicating feelings and ideas,” Hartman says. Maybe you give them one-word or noncommittal answers, or you share less with them about your day than you normally would. You may even start to shut down or hide your true thoughts from them, which can create emotional distance (and resentment) over time. On that note...

“When someone is falling out of love, they may become emotionally distant, avoid physical intimacy, stop expressing affection, or appear detached and unresponsive to emotional needs,” says Brandon Santan, PhD, a therapist specializing in relationships based in Chattanooga, Tennessee.

Essentially, you're operating on autopilot—you're going through all the motions but (pardon the pun) your heart's not in it. “A noticeable decline in enthusiasm and interest in spending time together, engaging in shared activities, or discussing future plans may suggest that feelings are fading,” Santan says.

Conflict is a natural part of relationships, but sometimes, too much friction means love is fizzling. “When there is more conflict than connection, it is time to look at and address what has changed and get curious about why that is,” Coleman says.

A partner who is falling out of love may also instigate conflict as a means of creating distance in the relationship, adds Manly, but it’s even more telling when there’s no interest in making peace at all. “Although ongoing conflicts are never a good sign in a relationship, a lack of interest in resolving the root issues is often a sign that feelings of love have waned—or that there was an inability to create lasting love from the onset,” she says.

6. You’re not as interested in quality time as you used to be.

Thoughtfully-planned date nights and spontaneous weekend trips may have been the norm when you first got together, but if you’re significantly less eager to spend QT with your S.O. nowadays, it could be a red flag. “When you find reasons to not be around [your partner], it’s a signal that something is amiss in the relationship,” says Saba Harouni Lurie, LMFT, a licensed marriage and family therapist and owner of Take Root Therapy in Los Angeles. Maybe you’re“too busy with work” for your usual weeknight hang, or you’re suddenly too sick to attend an important family event with them.

The disconnect can apply to quieter everyday moments, too. “When you don't feel excited about your partner or the idea of spending time with them—especially doing the mundane tasks of life—it is likely that you are not feeling as in love anymore,” says Coleman.

7. Intimacy and sex are minimal or nonexistent.

There are many different types of intimacy (including non-physical) that contribute to a strong connection. But when intimacy no longer exists, it can feel impossible to experience love. “If the idea of physical touch or intimacy feels like something you'd rather not do and you don't have much desire to engage in it, this could be a sign [you're falling out of love],” says Surabhi Jagdish, a licensed marriage and family therapist in Houston.

There are many reasons your (or your partner’s) sex drive might ebb and flow, and having sex or being intimate less frequently doesn’t automatically mean you’re falling out of love. But it can indicate that something is off—especially if there’s a complete lack of communication, and no interest in fostering other forms of connection.

“Partners who are deeply connected on an emotional level tend to move through the natural highs and lows of sexual intimacy with grace,” Manly says.

This one might seem painfully obvious, but if you often fantasize about being single, feel jealous of your unattached friends, or wonder what it would be like to date other people, it's a pretty clear indication that you are contemplating a life outside the relationship, Santan says.

Now, there's not necessarily a cause for concern if you simply have a crush on someone who's not your partner—these occasionally happen in long-term monogamous relationships, and they're generally harmless. But if you’re regularly entertaining the idea of life without your partner to the point where you think you’d be better off without them, you may have already made up your mind.

It’s normal to get on each other’s nerves a little—nobody is perfect! But maybe you constantly feel frustrated by the way they chew their food, leave their clothes on the floor, or talk through TV shows. If your partner’s quirks, behavior, and personality seem to get on your nerves more than usual, or if something you once found endearing is now annoying, it’s likely a sign you're no longer feeling the love, says Manly.

Falling out of love may happen naturally, due to partners taking different life paths that feel very separate. Say your partner enjoys traveling extensively for work, yet you constantly crave more time together. After years of ongoing separation and lack of physical connection, the relationship may ultimately suffer, Manly says.

“Loving partnerships rely on communication, togetherness, shared ventures, and connective play,” she adds. “If partners routinely take paths that leave each person feeling separate and alone, the loving bonds often fade away.” To close the gap between diverging life paths, you might make a concerted effort to be with your partner by always traveling to meet up on their business trips, for example. But if that effort is not reciprocated, you may to start to feel continuously “unseen or unloved,” says Manly, which can lead to someone falling out of love.

Falling out of love or... just feeling comfortable? Over time, as a couple becomes comfortable in their relationship, they may feel less passion and lust for one another than they did in the beginning. Instead, they develop a more companionate relationship, where they feel less of the highs (and lows) of an uber romantic feeling of love, and more of a steady and safe friendship feeling of love. “You are still invested and connected. There’s a feeling of love and desire…it just changes,” Harouni Lurie says. For example, you may not have sex as often as in the early days of dating; instead, you find contentment cozying up on the couch and watching TV together. That doesn’t mean you’re not in love anymore—it’s actually normal and healthy for relationships to feel more companionate over time. “It’s not sustainable to stay in lust and to have the passion we experienced when first falling in love,” says Harouni Lurie. “We wouldn’t be able to live our lives as fully if we were always in that place.” When you're falling out of love, however, you don't feel comfortable—you feel stuck. Figuring out where you feelings lie will help you decide what to do next.

Before making any big decisions about your relationship, it's necessary to understand what’s causing this newfound lack of love, Harouni Lurie says. Is it circumstantial, like one person working too much? Was there a betrayal that never got addressed and you slowly drifted apart?

There could be a single make-or-break issue, but usually, there’s more than one factor, says Sarah Epstein, a licensed marriage and family therapist who practices in Philadelphia and Dallas. What’s more: The cause(s) might be out of your control. “In some cases, the issue has nothing to do with a partner’s behavior and is solely related to stress, unresolved internal issues, or personal shifts,” Manly says.

It’s also possible that you’re not falling out of love, you’re just feeling disconnected, Epstein adds. Maybe you’re in different life stages—for example, your partner is starting grad school, while you’re trying to climb the corporate ladder—so you don’t feel like you have much in common anymore. Your lives, which were once intertwined, now seem to be going in different directions.

Once you know the root of the issue, consider what you need to rekindle your connection, Epstein says. There isn’t a one-size-fits-all approach when it comes to reviving a relationship, but self-reflection is often a key component, says Manly. That might look like journaling, pursuing a passion, or seeing a therapist.

That includes assessing shared values and long-term goals. “Determine whether the issues you are facing are temporary, or if they point to deeper incompatibilities that may be difficult to overcome,” Santan says. “This assessment can help you gain clarity on whether the relationship is salvageable, or if it may be healthier for both parties to move on.”

It can be tough to admit you’re falling out of love, and even trickier to communicate that to your partner. Even so, Santan suggests initiating an honest, compassionate conversation with them about your feelings. “Choose a suitable time and place to express your thoughts, concerns, and doubts,” he says. “Clearly communicate your emotional state and be open to listening to their perspective as well. Keep in mind that effective communication is key to understanding each other and finding potential solutions.”

You might say something like, “Hey, there’s something I’ve been wanting to talk to you about. Sometimes, it feels like our connection has changed… do you ever feel that way, too?” Admitting something super vulnerable—and potentially very hurtful—can be intimidating, but it's a necessary to get to the root of the issue. Plus, if your partner has been feeling the same way, the conversation can be a healing experience for you both.

From there, you've got options: You can decide that the relationship is repairable, call it quits, take a break to reassess further, or seek couples therapy to help you navigate the situation. “Based on the self-reflection, communication, and professional guidance, you will eventually need to make a decision about the future of your relationship,” Santan says. “Remember that this decision should be made with careful consideration of your own well-being and the well-being of your partner.”

“Falling back in love takes time and effort from both partners. It may not happen overnight, and patience, understanding, and commitment are key,” Santan says. “If both individuals are willing to put in the work and are open to growth, it is possible to rediscover the love and connection that initially brought them together.”

Remember, as you both grow and change as people, so must your relationship. “The goal is to find new ways to rediscover each other, and pursue the experience of being in love with them over and over again,” says Coleman.

“Take some time to ask yourself, ‘What attracted me to my partner? What helped me fall in love in the first place? What are some things I said or did but don't anymore? What are some things they said or did but don't do anymore? Are these things I still experience or notice with my partner?’” Jagdish says. “If you can identify these things, look for them in your relationship again.”

It all comes back to getting curious about your partner. Imagine going on a familiar hike—with an incurious mindset, you may not be as interested in your surroundings because you’ve been down this path before. However, “if you have a more curious mindset, you might say to yourself, ‘I know I’ve done this before, but it has evolved since the last time I’ve been here. There’s more to see, learn and cherish,’” Manly says. Maybe a boulder hasn’t moved since your last trek, but this time, you notice the way the mica in the rock sparkles in the sunlight, helping you appreciate its beauty all over again.

It’s useful for couples to think of their relationship not as one relationship, but as many different versions. “What your relationship looks like at 20 isn’t going to be what it looks like at 40, 60, etc.,” says Epstein. “Beliefs, values, trauma, all of these things will change your relationship over time.”

Instead of thinking, “How can we get back to what our relationship was?” reframing the question to: How can this relationship work for us now? What can our love and connection look like going forward?

Ultimately, whether you decide to rebuild or end the relationship, know that you deserve happiness and all the joy that lasting love can bring.

RealPRO Select helps lift Realtor.com revenue

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Realtor.com reported $160 million in fiscal Q1 2027 revenue, up 5% year over year, extending an eight-quarter growth streak. News Corp cited RealPRO Select expansion and broader RealAssist AI rollout as key drivers.  AI Summary

Move, the parent company of Realtor.com, said the momentum generated by RealPRO Select in fiscal year 2026 has carried over into the first quarter of fiscal year 2027.

During the first quarter of FY 2027, Realtor.com recorded $160 million in revenue, up 5% year over year, marking the segment’s eighth consecutive quarter of growth. These financial results were highlighted during News Corp’s fiscal year 2026 fourth-quarter earnings call on Wednesday evening.

“In a housing market constrained by 7%-plus mortgage rates, Damian [Eales] and the team have exhibited exceptional discipline in their operational focus and AI integration, and that approach has converted revenue growth into meaningful bottom-line expansion,” Robert Thomson, the CEO of News Corp, said during the earnings call.

News Corp executives attributed Realtor.com’s strong revenue performance to the growth of RealPRO Select. The company said it is focused on enhancing the platform with stronger lead generation, branding, productivity tools and training capabilities.

Realtor.com also focused on the expansion of RealAssist AI during Q1, including the launch of a pilot of RealAssist in Rich Communication Services (RCS), integrated directly into Google search and available on iOS and Android.

“The pilot is already attracting new users, driving strong engagement, and helping us capture pre-search intent earlier in the buyer journey before consumers ever enter a browser,” Damian Eales, the CEO of Move, wrote in a blog post about the company’s quarterly performance.

In addition, Realtor.com also launched RealAssist AI for Realtor.com+, which Eales said helps agents onboard their clients faster, build more tailored searches, summarize client activity and draft follow-ups in their own voice.

“The platform is already supporting thousands of successful agent-client connections, and early feedback on these AI tools has been very positive,” Eales wrote.

Eales also highlighted the relaunch of the My Home dashboard, which now includes a collaboration with Opendoor. Eales said this is encouraging sellers to engage a listing agent much earlier in their journey.

In addition, News Corp also noted that Comscore data shows 4.9 visits per unique user to Realtor.com, up 8% annually, and an average of 263 million monthly visits in the quarter, which the company said represents a “substantial” lead over Redfin and Homes.com.

Despite these strong numbers, Realtor.com noted that lead volume was down 11%, which the portal attributed to broader market conditions and its repositioning of customer acquisitions to higher-quality and higher-value leads.

“Despite a constrained housing market, Realtor.com’s strategy is delivering positive results, and we’re well positioned to grow as conditions improve,” Eales wrote. “Our focus remains on investing in our audience and AI tools that enhance a consumer’s experience and augment the role of the professional agent.”

Overall, News Corp’s digital real estate services segment generated $529 million in revenue, up 10% from a year prior. The company as a whole generated $2.28 billion in revenue, a 6% annual increase, and net income of $215 million, up 43% compared to a year prior.

United’s Premium Boeing 787s With New Cabins: Which Routes Do They Fly?

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

We recently saw United Airlines introduce its long awaited new Boeing 787-9s, featuring all-new cabins, which are being branded as United Elevate. This represents the evolution of the carrier’s long haul experience, including all-new Polaris business class seats.

Of course one common question is which routes actually feature these new planes. So in this post, I’d like to go over that — which planes have the new cabins, and which routes do they fly? The airline is flying these planes on an increasing number of routes out of two hubs, so there are more opportunities to try this product.

Prior to 2026, United already had over 80 Boeing 787 Dreamliners in its fleet, split between the 787-8, 787-9, and 787-10 variants. However, United is now taking delivery of another 71 Boeing 787-9s, which recently started to be delivered (in addition to 56 Boeing 787-10s, which will be delivered as of 2028).

What’s exciting about these planes is that they feature an all-new passenger experience, and are in an ultra premium configuration. The planes feature just 222 seats, compared to the 257 seats you’ll find on all the previous 787-9s.

If you want to actually fly a United Boeing 787-9 with the new United Elevate interiors, what are your best odds of getting on one of these? The good news is that they’re consistently scheduled on certain routes.

As it currently stands, United has 16 of these 787s in its fleet, with several more expected to be delivered in the coming months. United plans to have 33 of these jets by 2028, so the airline is taking delivery of an average of around one of these per month (though the pace has been even faster in the first several months).

When you go to book a ticket, the best way to tell if a flight is operated by the new plane is whether you see “Polaris Studio available” listed under the United Polaris fare options (assuming those seats are for sale). Otherwise there are no obvious indicators, other than looking at the seat map.

We recently saw the IATA winter season kick off, and with that, we’re seeing the plane based out of both Houston (IAH) and San Francisco (SFO).

You’ll currently find the 787 with Elevate interiors scheduled to fly to the following destinations out of San Francisco:

You’ll then find the 787 with Elevate interiors scheduled to fly to the following destinations out of Houston:

Let me emphasize that schedules are always subject to change (especially when Boeing delivery delays are a potential factor), and the above is just how things currently stand. I’m also not including flights between San Francisco and Houston, which sometimes see the plane so that it can be rotated between hubs, but obviously that route doesn’t feature the true Polaris soft product.

In recent months, we’ve seen United start to take delivery of its brand new Boeing 787-9s, featuring a more premium layout, plus new cabins. These are exciting planes for passengers, as they feature a much improved onboard experience. They’re also great in terms of upgrade and award availability, given the number of premium seats.

If you want to fly United’s new 787s, you’ll currently find them flying out of Houston and San Francisco. Out of Houston, they’re primarily flying to London, Sao Paulo, Sydney, and Tokyo, while out of San Francisco, they’re primarily flying to London and Singapore. It’s great to see the pace at which United is taking delivery of these planes.

I look forward to trying the new interiors, though I’m hoping to wait until these planes have Starlink installed, since I think that really completes the experience.

And I have to say, the touchpad for operating the seat and lights was not very intuitive to me and I like to think I'm pretty savvy with those things. And apparently for some of the new planes, the wired headphone jack didn't work at my seat (taped over). The gate agent in SIN and flight attendant were very proactive in telling me the bluetooth worked (once I figured out how to pair it) and the gate agent gave me 7500 miles as compensation. Food was decent, didn't blow me away, but very good, even the chicken. The crew also locked the exterior windows to keep it dark. Overall, the seat is quite comfortable and the flight crew was excellent (though the purser seemed stretched and if you weren't GS or 1K, I wasn't on his radar). Would be nice if purser didn't exclude regular pax in biz when thanking them for flying and loyalty.

Brighton goalkeeper Bart Verbruggen dismissed recent reports linking him with a move to La Liga.

Verbruggen, 22, was on media duties with head coach Fabian Hurzeler ahead of their Conference League clash at Madrid-based club Getafe.

9 Curb Appeal Trends That Will Take Over in 2027, According to Designers

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

These are the exterior elements that are getting a major refresh.

Every item on this page was hand-picked by a House Beautiful editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Curb appeal is more than having a pretty house.

“It's not just about how a home looks, but how it lives,” says Danielle Faustini, landscape designer and owner of Helix Green Designs. Homeowners and designers alike are stepping up their game when it comes to creating an appealing facade—in some cases, the shift is quite literal, as stairs are becoming an “it” feature.

The latest U.S. Houzz Outdoor Trends study is seeing a shift toward natural elements like wood cladding, beige exteriors, and native plants.

"Professionals on Houzz have shared that homeowners are looking for exteriors that feel grounded and connected to their surroundings," says Annie Thornton, Houzz outdoor trends expert. “Warm beiges and wood siding blend naturally with stone, native plantings and other organic landscape materials, and they suit a wide range of architectural styles. Our studies show that people are moving away from the stark white and cool gray palettes of recent years toward something that feels more timeless, a look that adds a sense of history without tipping into nostalgia.”

Simple upgrades, like a fresh coat of paint on the front door, are also pivoting into colorways anchored in the past. Designer Alexis Warren is seeing a shift toward darker, moodier front doors in shades of green, deep purple, and near-black, like Farrow & Ball’s Paean Black.

Here are 10 curb-appeal-boosting design trends for the year ahead to make your house the envy of the neighborhood.

Introducing a sculptural element—like an outdoor artwork or creatively placed flora—sends a message that your house has a point of view.

“It elevates the sense of arrival and separates your home from other homes in the vicinity,” says designer Lisa Erdmann. To ensure your home truly stands out, look for one-of-a-kind pieces.

For the home pictured above, Robert J. Bell, founder of landscape architecture firm Bell Design, commissioned artisans to create a copper light inspired by a feather. “More than the end of minimalism, the new focus on sculptural shapes brings back a sense of craft, and the expressive and sensory nature that a simple black metal and glass fixture cannot possess,” he says.

To contrast against the patina, Bell chose purple bougainvillea and a sunny yellow paint on the door.

It’s all about adding warmth and texture, and that’s one of the reasons that beige exterior colors have more than doubled in popularity in the last two years, jumping from 10 to 24 percent, according to Houzz.

Think of a palette that extends into the landscape—warm neutrals such as tan, soft cream, khaki, sand, and greige, says Thornton, who also notes that natural wood and wood-look cladding, sandy stucco, and stone are a welcome change.

Adding paneling can mimic the warmth of wood without worrying about upkeep, points out Francisca Alonso, cofounder and CEO of AV Architects + Builders. This trick also helps soften the front elevation. “We like to combine minimalistic modern details with warm tones to make it more inviting and less commercial-looking,” Alonso adds.

A picket fence may be just what your house needs, but that doesn’t mean you have to resort to the predictable white paint.

“Adding a low-profile picket fence is one of the best ways to gently delineate a property line without in any way compromising sightlines of the home,” says Joelle Nesen, principal designer at Maison Inc. “The picket fence has the kind of classical lines that work well with an historic home like this Craftsman in Portland. By not defaulting to the expected white and leaving the fence a natural wood, the picket fence, with its carefully spaced slats that are almost see-through, allows it to blend in with the landscaping and trees that surround the home.”

When you choose gravel over grass or pavers, it’s all about an understated but classy look.

“Gravel and other permeable aggregates like crushed shells are some of my favorite materials for creating more informal outdoor living spaces,” says Faustini. “It is a beautifully tactile and sensory material. Who doesn't love the soft crunch underfoot? And it really softens a space, helping blur the line between outdoor living areas and the surrounding landscape.”

The look works especially well with decking and patinated light fixtures.

Adding native plants around your house feels cohesive, relaxed, and rooted in place, Thornton says.

Rachael Grochowski, founder and principal of RHG Architecture + Design, adds that she expects to see an increase in native landscapes, meadow-type gardens, as well as an integration of clover into lawns.

“The trend to embrace natural landscapes is not only about supporting environment regeneration, but it also supports our wellbeing by instigating wonder, beauty, and randomness,” she says.

Curb appeal is all about first impressions, and homeowners are not taking this feature lightly. Houzz reports that searches for “front walkway” have more than quadrupled (up 357 percent) in the first three quarters of 2026, compared with the same period last year.

Step design, in particular, is one feature that is growing in popularity.

When Sarah Crawford, associate designer with Courtney B. Smith Design, renovated her home, she added steps to her 1920s bungalow to literally and figuratively elevate the experience. The home initially had just one step from the sidewalk to the front door. By adding a series of stairs to gently guide the way to the door, she added a quiet sense of elegance in keeping with the wood-shingled exterior. Key to the transformation was selecting materials that reflect the character and style of the original home, in this case reclaimed granite. Scale and balance were essential to adding a series of stairs to gently guide visitors to the door.

“Steps should never be narrower than the doorway,” Crawford says. “In fact, the wider the steps, the more inviting the approach.”

For a different approach to the front entrance, consider stone pathways, especially those with a casual look, like grass pavers.

Interior designer Darci Hether likes large-format bluestone pavers, which offer a clean, timeless look. “The generous scale of the stones creates a sense of openness, while the greenery between them softens the overall appearance and adds a beautiful, natural element,” she explains.

Erdmann encourages her clients to forgo safe neutrals and treat the front entry door as a place for personality.

“Whether it is hot pink, lipstick red, cobalt blue, or Benjamin Moore’s color of the year, Webster Green—make a statement!” she says. “Don’t match the door to the shutters and choose something fun. A front door is the easiest, lowest-risk place to experiment since it takes one afternoon and a quart of paint.”

Case in point, Elizabeth Cross-Beard, interior designer behind Palm Henri, took to a vibrant peacock blue to accentuate the lines of the above 1925 French manor house. “When paired with natural materials and muted tones, [the paint] brings just enough color to intrigue the eye without disrupting the overall sense of classic grandeur. Unlike other blue tones which can call too much attention to them, this rich color is the front-door sweet spot,” Cross-Beard explains.

Go beyond ho-hum pots that stand sentry at your door. Look for large pieces with personality to anchor the entrance.

“Aged terracotta, weathered stone, or antique urns bring patina and character, helping even a newly built home feel established,” says MK Smith Boykin of Smith Interiors. “With that kind of presence, the planting itself can be quite simple.” The details are especially important when your home has architectural symmetry, like columns.

“Using matching plants only increases the allure of perfect visual balance,” Nesen adds. “The key is that the scale of the planters needs to match the proportions of the door and porch: Selecting pots or containers that are too small will immediately drain the drama and beauty of the front door statement.”

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/payers/nabip-sues-cms-over-moratorium-new-aca-broker-registrations" hreflang="en">Court blocks CMS' moratorium on new ACA broker registrations</a>

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →
Court blocks CMS' moratorium on new ACA broker registrations" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

A federal court has granted a preliminary injunction to two key groups representing insurance brokers over the Trump administration's moratorium on new broker enrollments.

The District Court of Maryland issued the injunction after determining that the National Association of Benefits and Insurance Professionals and Health Agents for America "have shown that they are likely to succeed on the merits of their claims under the Administrative Procedure Act."

The court also said that the organizations made clear they would likely suffer significant harm if the moratorium stays in place.

NABIP said that the ruling means that it will restart training for new brokers beginning on Nov. 6.

"Today’s ruling is a significant victory for licensed agents and brokers and the consumers who rely on their expertise," said NABIP President Mychal Walker in an announcement (PDF). "With Open Enrollment already underway, every day matters."

"Lifting the moratorium immediately and beginning training tomorrow will help affected professionals get to work assisting individuals and families in finding coverage that fits their needs and budgets," said Walker.

NABIP initially sued on Oct. 15 to challenge the moratorium. The Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services instituted the moratorium in late September as part of its broader push to combat fraud on the Affordable Care Act's exchanges.

The National Association of Benefits and Insurance Professionals is suing the Trump administration over its moratorium on new brokers entering the Affordable Care Act marketplaces.

The organization, the leading trade group for insurance brokers and agents, said the Centers for Medicare and Medicaid Services' moratorium impacts close to 19,000 brokers and agents who will be barred from registering ahead of open enrollment.

CMS unveiled the moratorium in late September as part of its ongoing push to root out fraud, waste and abuse. In addition, the agency said it would terminate more than 200 agents and brokers that failed to comply with marketplace standards.

The agency said new brokers and agents were disproportionately responsible for submitting inappropriate enrollments and engaging in behaviors at high risk for fraud.

In an announcement (PDF), NABIP says that it supports the broader effort to combat fraud, but that CMS' moratorium "threatens the livelihoods of thousands of legitimate, licensed professionals who have not been accused of fraud and limits consumers’ access to professional assistance as they navigate Open Enrollment."

The organization argues in the lawsuit that the rule instituting the moratorium violates both the Administrative Procedure Act and the Affordable Care Act. The regulation is unlawful as it is "arbitrary, capricious, and unsupported by sufficient evidence," NABIP said.

The 19,000 agents who would be barred from participating under the rule are part of a critical network of professionals who help consumers find coverage that meets their needs, the organization said.

NABIP President Mychal Walker said that CMS should look elsewhere in its fraud efforts.

"The existence of fraud is not in dispute. NABIP has raised these concerns with CMS repeatedly and has offered concrete solutions to address them," Walker said in the announcement. "What is in dispute is whether the appropriate response is to exclude thousands of legitimate agents based solely on whether they registered last year, without any individualized evidence about them."

"There is a better approach: target fraud where it occurs, strengthen the system’s security and hold bad actors accountable while preserving consumers’ access to trusted professionals," Walker continued.

CMS has taken steps to crack down on brokers as the agency views improper ACA enrollments as a key target in its fight against fraud. CMS officials have repeatedly raised concerns that people are enrolled in ACA plans without their knowledge, or despite being deceased.

Earlier this week, the agency revealed it will purge 189,000 enrollees who are currently listed as enrolled in both ACA plans and other coverage, such as Medicaid or Medicare.

Best Decentralized Video Streaming Platforms: 5 YouTube Alternatives

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

Home » Cryptopedia » Best Decentralized Video Streaming Platforms: 5 YouTube Alternatives

Explore the top decentralized video platforms, compare their features, and discover alternatives that give creators more control and freedom.

YouTube dominates online video, but it isn’t the only option for watching, sharing, and publishing content. Decentralized video platforms offer an alternative model, giving creators and communities more control over content distribution, hosting, and, in some cases, monetization.

Unlike traditional video platforms that rely on centralized infrastructure, decentralized services can use peer-to-peer networks, blockchain technology, or federated servers to distribute and manage video content.

But which platforms are actually worth using?

In this guide, we explore seven decentralized video platforms and YouTube alternatives, including DTube, Odysee, PeerTube, 3Speak, DLive, Theta, and Flixxo. We’ll compare their underlying technology, publishing features, monetization options, and limitations to help you choose the right platform.

Decentralized video platforms distribute video hosting, storage, and delivery across independent servers or networks rather than relying on a single company like YouTube. This gives creators and communities more control over content distribution, ownership, and monetization.

They use different technologies. Blockchain records transactions and can reward creators with tokens. Peer-to-peer (P2P) networks allow viewers to share video data directly with one another. Federated platforms, such as PeerTube, connect independently operated servers that share content through protocols like ActivityPub.

Decentralized video hosting works by storing videos across multiple servers or distributed networks. When users watch content, data can be delivered from different sources rather than one central provider.

However, not all decentralized YouTube alternatives are fully independent or censorship-resistant. Some still rely on centralized services for hosting, moderation, payments, or content discovery.

As the digital landscape evolves, several decentralized platforms are rising to the challenge, offering compelling alternatives to YouTube. Without further ado, here are our top seven decentralized YouTube alternatives.

DTube is a decentralized video-sharing platform that lets users upload, watch, and share videos without paying subscription fees. Unlike traditional video platforms, it uses peer-to-peer (P2P) technology to distribute video data between viewers, reducing reliance on centralized streaming infrastructure.

Its P2P model makes it an interesting alternative for users who value more distributed video delivery.

Odisee is an open-source video-sharing platform built on the LBRY protocol, a decentralized network for publishing and distributing digital content.

Unlike conventional video services, LBRY uses blockchain technology to register content claims and a peer-to-peer network to distribute files. Odysee provides a user-friendly interface for accessing videos and other digital media across this ecosystem.

Creators can publish videos, set prices for paid content, and receive tips or eligible viewing rewards in LBRY Credits (LBC). Publishing requires Credits, while watching content is generally free. Importantly, Odysee and LBRY are not interchangeable: Odysee is one application built on the broader LBRY protocol, and other compatible applications can access its content.

The platform offers users a range of options for creating, sharing, uploading, downloading, and purchasing content. It also rewards users with its own cryptocurrency, LBRY Credits (LBC), for various activities on the platform.

PeerTube is a free, open-source video-sharing platform that offers an alternative to centralized services like YouTube. Instead of relying on one company to host all content, it operates through independent servers called instances. These servers can connect and share videos through the ActivityPub protocol, allowing users to discover and interact with content across a federated network.

PeerTube supports video uploads, livestreaming, subscriptions, and community interaction. It also uses optional peer-to-peer streaming to reduce server bandwidth by allowing viewers to share video data.

The platform’s open-source architecture gives communities and organizations control over their own hosting environments, although content discovery and moderation can vary between instances.

3Speak is a decentralized YouTube alternative built on the Hive blockchain, designed to give creators greater ownership of their content.

Videos are published as posts under creators’ Hive accounts, while video files are stored on IPFS. This allows content to remain accessible through compatible Hive applications even if it is no longer displayed on the 3Speak website.

The platform offers long-form videos, Shorts, communities, and podcast RSS feeds. Creators can earn HIVE and HBD through community upvotes and receive a share of advertising revenue.

Viewers can also qualify for rewards by watching videos, podcasts or participating in content curation. These features make 3Speak particularly relevant to creators interested in blockchain-based publishing and alternative monetization models.

If you’ve ever used Netflix before (let’s be honest, everyone has) Flixxo’s UI will already be familiar to you.

Flixxo is a video platform focused on independent short films and microseries from emerging creators. Rather than competing directly with YouTube’s broad content library, it offers a curated entertainment experience featuring original stories and independent productions.

The platform focuses on helping creators distribute and monetize their work while giving viewers access to content from new voices. Its discovery model emphasizes editorial curation rather than relying entirely on recommendation algorithms.

Flixxo is therefore better suited to viewers interested in independent entertainment and short-form storytelling than those looking for a general-purpose video-sharing platform.

Traditional platforms like YouTube rely on centralized servers controlled by a single company. Decentralized video platforms distribute hosting and content delivery across independent servers, peer-to-peer (P2P) networks, or distributed storage systems.

Videos can be stored using technologies such as IPFS or hosted on independent servers. P2P networks allow viewers to share video data directly, reducing reliance on a single provider. Some platforms also use blockchain technology to record transactions, manage content ownership, and reward creators through cryptocurrency tokens.

Not every decentralized platform uses blockchain or crypto. Their technologies and monetization models vary depending on the underlying architecture.

Decentralized YouTube alternatives offer a new and innovative approach to video streaming, but like all technologies, they come with their own set of pros and cons.

With so many decentralized YouTube alternatives out there, it’s hard to know which are providing a quality service worth using. This guide aims to narrow your search and help you find a decentralized video platform that suits your needs.

Yes. Platforms like PeerTube, Odysee, and DTube offer alternatives to YouTube using decentralized technologies such as peer-to-peer networks, blockchain, or federated servers. They give users more control over content distribution and hosting.

It depends on your needs. PeerTube is a strong choice for open-source, federated video hosting, Odysee suits independent content publishing, while 3Speak focuses on blockchain-based creator rewards.

On decentralized YouTube alternatives, the platform is owned and governed by its users. These platforms are often backed by crypto economies that reward user engagement and interaction.

Yes. Some platforms support cryptocurrency rewards, tips, paid content, or advertising revenue sharing. For example, Odysee supports LBRY Credits (LBC), while 3Speak offers Hive-based rewards. Monetization options vary by platform.

They can be more resistant to centralized censorship because content is distributed across independent servers or networks. However, individual platform operators and server administrators can still moderate or remove content.

Some do, but not all. Odysee uses the LBRY blockchain ecosystem, while 3Speak integrates with Hive. PeerTube primarily relies on federation and peer-to-peer technology rather than blockchain.

Japanese Charts: Switch 2 Sales Get A Nice Bump Ahead Of Zelda: Ocarina Of Time

📅 2026-11-05  |  Read Source →

We are back with another look at the Japanese charts, courtesy of Famitsu, and it has been another big week for Nintendo.

On the software side of things, Nintendo Switch Sports Resort continues to lead the charge, holding onto its pole position from last week with another ~23,000 units to its name. The Switch 2 edition of Minecraft also makes its chart debut this time, grabbing a podium spot at third.

Elsewhere, Final Fantasy Resonance continues to perform well on both Switch 2 and PS5, and Hello Kitty Party Land has made its chart debut in seventh and eighth on Switch 2 and 1 respectively.

The Nintendo big hitter this week comes on the hardware end, where Switch 2 has seen a decent bump from the ~mid-20,000s we usually see to a meaty 68,733 sales. We can only assume that this is thanks to the swanky new Zelda Special Edition model and in anticipation of Ocarina of Time, which, when these numbers were recorded, was only a matter of days away.

The PS5 family once again wins the combined SKU race this week, coming in with a total of 16,235, while the Switch 1 followed behind with 5,853 weekly sales.
